    "file": {
      "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_2/projects/entrainment_gated_ft/figures/paper/gen_fig5_per_example_bars.py",
      "content": "import matplotlib\nmatplotlib.use('Agg')\nimport matplotlib.pyplot as plt\nimport numpy as np\nimport os\n\nplt.rcParams.update({\n    'font.family': 'DejaVu Sans',\n    'font.size': 11,\n    'axes.titlesize': 12,\n    'axes.labelsize': 12,\n    'xtick.labelsize': 10,\n    'ytick.labelsize': 10,\n    'legend.fontsize': 9,\n    'figure.dpi': 300,\n    'savefig.dpi': 300,\n    'savefig.bbox': 'tight',\n    'savefig.pad_inches': 0.05,\n    'axes.spines.top': False,\n    'axes.spines.right': False,\n    'pdf.fonttype': 42,\n    'ps.fonttype': 42,\n})\n\n# Data from per_example_table.csv (LAMBADA, 1.3B, h3-1 vs pure)\narchs = ['GDN', 'RetNet', 'HGRN2']\nhelped = [462, 426, 548]\nhurt = [615, 495, 395]\nnet = [-153, -69, 153]\n\nx = np.arange(len(archs))\nwidth = 0.35\n\nfig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(3.3, 2.8))\n\nbars_h = ax.bar(x - width/2, helped, width, label='Helped', color='#2ca02c', alpha=0.85, zorder=3)\nbars_u = ax.bar(x + width/2, hurt, width, label='Hurt', color='#d62728', alpha=0.85, zorder=3)\n\n# Net effect annotations\nfor i, (h, u, n) in enumerate(zip(helped, hurt, net)):\n    y_max = max(h, u) + 15\n    sign = '+' if n > 0 else ''\n    color = '#2ca02c' if n > 0 else '#d62728'\n    ax.text(i, y_max, f'net {sign}{n}', ha='center', va='bottom',\n            fontsize=9, fontweight='bold', color=color)\n\nax.set_xticks(x)\nax.set_xticklabels(archs)\nax.set_ylabel('Number of examples')\nax.set_ylim(0, 720)\nax.legend(loc='lower right', framealpha=0.9)\n\nax.text(0.98, 0.97, 'LAMBADA\\n$N = 5{,}153$, 1.3B',\n        transform=ax.transAxes, fontsize=8, fontstyle='italic',\n        color='#555555', va='top', ha='right')\n\nout_dir = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))\nfig.savefig(os.path.join(out_dir, 'fig5_per_example_bars.pdf'))\nfig.savefig(os.path.join(out_dir, 'fig5_per_example_bars.png'), dpi=300)\nplt.close()\nprint('Saved fig5_per_example_bars')\n",

      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 62
    }

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I have a clear visual picture of all the figures. Let me also look at the heatmap PDF to visually inspect it. Let me convert it to PNG first.

Let me diagnose each figure:

**Figure 1 (Hero) - Decoupling:**
- Overall decent but can be improved
- Issues:
  - The panel b CI bands are extremely wide and visually dominant (the shaded gray bands are too thick and dominant)
  - The "saturation zone" label is awkwardly placed below the data
  - Panel widths are asymmetric but could be better balanced
  - The "rebound" annotation feels cluttered alongside the -79% label, which could be presented more elegantly
  - Panel b's x-axis labels should align with panel a to maintain consistency
  - The decoupling insight isn't clearly communicated through the visual structure
  - Adding more visual distinction between the panels would strengthen the key message

**Figure 2 - Task Specificity (Spearman rho lollipop):**
- The legend placement outside the figure creates width issues that could cause problems in single-column layouts
- Some p-values are overlapping with the markers

, particularly around the 0.6 range
- The green color for comprehension doesn't match the project's palette and feels like a default matplotlib choice
- Dataset names like "xstorycloze_en" need cleaner formatting
- The annotation text is oversized and adds visual noise, while the background shading could use more muted tones

**Figure 3 (Heatmap):**
- Still waiting to convert the PDF to PNG from the remote server

**Figure 4 - Jaccard Saturation:**
- The overlapping lines make it difficult to distinguish individual traces, and the color palette needs better alignment with the project standards
- The gray saturation band is barely visible and the legend placement needs adjustment
- The Y-axis could provide more context by showing the independence baseline rather than just starting at 0.05
- Direct labels on the lines would be cleaner than relying on the legend

**Figure 5 - Jaccard Hierarchy:**
- The traffic-light color scheme (gray, red, yellow, green) doesn't match the project palette, and the red for "Cross-arch." is semantically misleading since it's the main finding, not a problem
- The bracket annotations work well, but the value labels use bar colors that create readability problems, especially yellow and red on white backgrounds
- Now converting the heatmap PDF to examine it, and I notice the task spec references a specific file name for Figure 3 that doesn't match what the Python script generates locally—the heatmap was created on the remote server instead

I need to plan the optimization across all five figures: refine Figure 1's layout and annotations, fix Figure 2's colors and naming, recreate Figure 3 locally since it came from the remote server, add baselines and direct labels to Figure 4, and realign Figure 5's palette with the project colors. Let me start by inspecting the heatmap PDF from the paper figures directory.

'legend.fontsize': 6.5,\n    'figure.dpi': 300,\n    'savefig.dpi': 300,\n    'savefig.bbox': 'tight',\n    'savefig.pad_inches': 0.05,\n    'axes.spines.top': False,\n    'axes.spines.right': False,\n    'pdf.fonttype': 42,\n    'ps.fonttype': 42,\n    'axes.linewidth': 0.6,\n    'xtick.major.width': 0.5,\n    'ytick.major.width': 0.5,\n})\n\ndata = [\n    ('LAMBADA',       'retrieval',      -1.0,  0.0167),\n    ('HellaSwag',     'reasoning',       0.3,  0.683),\n    ('ARC-Challenge', 'reasoning',       0.6,  0.35),\n    ('WinoGrande',    'reasoning',       0.1,  0.95),\n    ('ARC-Easy',      'mixed',          -0.7,  0.2333),\n    ('MMLU',          'mixed',          -0.7,  0.2333),\n    ('BoolQ',         'comprehension',   0.2,  0.7833),\n    ('PIQA',          'reasoning',      -0.5,  0.45),\n    ('SciQ',          'mixed',           0.1,  0.95),\n    ('XStoryCloze',   'mixed',          -0.6,  0.35),\n]\n\nbenchmarks = [d[0] for d in data]\ntaxonomies = [d[1] for d in data]\nrhos = [d[2] for d in data]\npvals = [d[3] for d in data]\n\norder = np.argsort(rhos)\nbenchmarks = [benchmarks[i] for i in order]\ntaxonomies = [taxonomies[i] for i in order]\nrhos = [rhos[i] for i in order]\npvals = [pvals[i] for i in order]\n\ntax_colors = {\n    'retrieval': '#0072B2',\n    'reasoning': '#E69F00',\n    'comprehension': '#009E73',\n    'mixed': '#999999',\n}\n\nfig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(3.3, 3.2))\n\nax.axvspan(-1.15, 0, color='#e8f5e9', alpha=0.35, zorder=0)\nax.axvspan(0, 0.75, color='#fbe9e7', alpha=0.35, zorder=0)\n\nt_conv = ax.text(-0.57, -0.45, 'convergence',\n                 fontsize=6.5, fontstyle='italic', color='#4a7c59',\n                 ha='center', va='bottom')\nt_conv.set_path_effects([pe.withStroke(linewidth=2, foreground='white')])\nt_div = ax.text(0.37, -0.45, 'divergence',\n                fontsize=6.5, fontstyle='italic', color='#a4161a',\n                ha='center', va='bottom')\nt_div.set_path_effects([pe.withStroke(linewidth=2, foreground='white')])\n\nax.axvline(0, color='#bbbbbb', linewidth=0.5, linestyle='--', zorder=1)\n\ny_positions = np.arange(len(benchmarks))\n\nfor i, (bench, tax, rho, p) in enumerate(zip(benchmarks, taxonomies, rhos, pvals)):\n    color = tax_colors[tax]\n    is_lambada = bench == 'LAMBADA'\n\n    ax.plot([0, rho], [i, i], '-', color=color, linewidth=0.8, alpha=0.4, zorder=2)\n\n    if is_lambada:\n        ax.plot(rho, i, 'o', color=color, markersize=6, zorder=5,\n                markeredgecolor='white', markeredgewidth=0.5)\n    else:\n        ax.plot(rho, i, 'o', markersize=4.5, zorder=4,\n                markerfacecolor='white', markeredgecolor=color, markeredgewidth=1.0)\n\n    if is_lambada:\n        label = f'$\\\\rho$ = {rho:.1f}, p = .017'\n        t = ax.text(0.72, i, label, fontsize=7, fontweight='bold',\n                    color=color, va='center', ha='right')\n    else:\n        p_str = f'{p:.3f}'.lstrip('0')\n        label = f'$\\\\rho$ = {rho:.1f}, p = {p_str}'\n        t = ax.text(0.72, i, label, fontsize=6, color='#777777',\n                    va='center', ha='right')\n    t.set_path_effects([pe.withStroke(linewidth=2, foreground='white')])\n\nax.set_yticks(y_positions)\nax.set_yticklabels(benchmarks, fontsize=7)\nax.set_xlim(-1.15, 0.75)\nax.set_ylim(-0.6, len(benchmarks) - 0.4)\nax.set_xlabel(r'Spearman $\\rho$  (attention ratio vs. cross-arch. $\\sigma$)')\nax.invert_yaxis()\n\nytick_labels = ax.get_yticklabels()\nfor label in ytick_labels:\n    if label.get_text() == 'LAMBADA':\n        label.set_fontweight('bold')\n        label.set_color(tax_colors['retrieval'])\n\nlegend_handles = [\n    mpatches.Patch(color='#0072B2', label='Retrieval'),\n    mpatches.Patch(color='#E69F00', label='Reasoning'),\n    mpatches.Patch(color='#009E73', label='Comprehension'),\n    mpatches.Patch(color='#999999', label='Mixed'),\n]\nax.legend(handles=legend_handles, loc='lower left',\n          framealpha=0.9, borderpad=0.3, handletextpad=0.4,\n          handlelength=1.0, fontsize=6.5)\n\nfig.text(0.5, -0.01,\n         'COPA ($n$=100) excluded; Bonferroni-adjusted $p$=.170 for LAMBADA.',\n         fontsize=5.5, fontstyle='italic', color='#999999', ha='center')\n\nout_dir = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))\npaper_fig_dir = '/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_2/projects/entrainment_gated_ft/docs/paper/figures'\nos.makedirs(paper_fig_dir, exist_ok=True)\n\nfor d in [out_dir, paper_fig_dir]:\n    fig.savefig(os.path.join(d, 'fig2_task_specificity.pdf'))\n    fig.savefig(os.path.join(d, 'fig2_task_specificity.png'), dpi=300)\nplt.close()\nprint('Saved fig2_task_specificity')\n"
}

'font.size': 11,\n    'axes.titlesize': 12,\n    'axes.labelsize': 12,\n    'xtick.labelsize': 10,\n    'ytick.labelsize': 10,\n    'legend.fontsize': 9,\n    'figure.dpi': 300,\n    'savefig.dpi': 300,\n    'savefig.bbox': 'tight',\n    'savefig.pad_inches': 0.05,\n    'axes.spines.top': False,\n    'axes.spines.right': False,\n    'pdf.fonttype': 42,\n    'ps.fonttype': 42,\n    'lines.linewidth': 1.8,\n})\n\n# Attention ratios (x-axis)\nratios = [0.04, 0.077, 0.143, 0.25]\nratio_labels = ['4%', '8%', '14%', '25%']\n\n# Mean Jaccard (avg of helped and hurt) per benchmark per ratio\n# Data from per-example analysis JSONs\ndata = {\n    'LAMBADA': {\n        'j_h': [0.0945, 0.0951, 0.0922, 0.1002],\n        'j_u': [0.0873, 0.0809, 0.0988, 0.0928],\n    },\n    'HellaSwag': {\n        'j_h': [0.0838, 0.0949, 0.0945, 0.0894],\n        'j_u': [0.0846, 0.0668, 0.0867, 0.0833],\n    },\n    'ARC-Easy': {\n        'j_h': [0.0748, 0.0981, 0.0935, 0.1023],\n        'j_u': [0.0934, 0.0994, 0.0876, 0.1149],\n    },\n    'PIQA': {\n        'j_h': [0.0852, 0.0862, 0.0807, 0.1122],\n        'j_u': [0.0931, 0.1050, 0.0976, 0.0784],\n    },\n}\n\n# Compute mean J per benchmark\nmean_j = {}\nfor bench, vals in data.items():\n    mean_j[bench] = [(h + u) / 2 for h, u in zip(vals['j_h'], vals['j_u'])]\n\ncolors = {\n    'LAMBADA': '#0072B2',\n    'HellaSwag': '#E69F00',\n    'ARC-Easy': '#999999',\n    'PIQA': '#D55E00',\n}\nmarkers = {\n    'LAMBADA': 'o',\n    'HellaSwag': 's',\n    'ARC-Easy': 'D',\n    'PIQA': '^',\n}\n\nfig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(3.3, 2.8))\n\nfor bench in ['LAMBADA', 'HellaSwag', 'ARC-Easy', 'PIQA']:\n    ax.plot(ratios, mean_j[bench], marker=markers[bench], color=colors[bench],\n            label=bench, markersize=5, zorder=3)\n\n# Shaded band showing the 0.08-0.10 range\nax.axhspan(0.08, 0.105, color='#cccccc', alpha=0.2, zorder=0)\n\nax.set_xlabel('Attention ratio')\nax.set_ylabel('Mean pairwise Jaccard $\\\\bar{J}$')\nax.set_xticks(ratios)\nax.set_xticklabels(ratio_labels)\nax.set_ylim(0.05, 0.13)\nax.legend(loc='upper right', framealpha=0.9, handlelength=1.5)\n\n# Add annotation\nax.text(0.16, 0.055, 'all $\\\\rho \\\\leq 0.80$, $p \\\\geq .333$',\n        fontsize=8, fontstyle='italic', color='#555555', ha='center')\n\nout_dir = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))\nfig.savefig(os.path.join(out_dir, 'fig4_jaccard_saturation.pdf'))\nfig.savefig(os.path.join(out_dir, 'fig4_jaccard_saturation.png'), dpi=300)\nplt.close()\nprint('Saved fig4_jaccard_saturation')\n",

    "originalFile": "import matplotlib\nmatplotlib.use('Agg')\nimport matplotlib.pyplot as plt\nimport numpy as np\nimport os\n\nplt.rcParams.update({\n    'font.family': 'DejaVu Sans',\n    'font.size': 11,\n    'axes.titlesize': 12,\n    'axes.labelsize': 12,\n    'xtick.labelsize': 9,\n    'ytick.labelsize': 10,\n    'legend.fontsize': 9,\n    'figure.dpi': 300,\n    'savefig.dpi': 300,\n    'savefig.bbox': 'tight',\n    'savefig.pad_inches': 0.05,\n    'axes.spines.top': False,\n    'axes.spines.right': False,\n    'pdf.fonttype': 42,\n    'ps.fonttype': 42,\n})\n\n# Four-tier Jaccard hierarchy (LAMBADA, 1.3B)\n# Each bar: mean of helped and hurt\ncategories = [\n    'Indep.\\nbaseline',\n    'Cross-\\narch.',\n    'Pythia\\nw/i seed',\n    'Within-\\narch.',\n]\n\n# Values (mean of helped and hurt J)\nmeans = [\n    0.05,                        # null baseline (permutation)\n    (0.096 + 0.091) / 2,         # cross-arch (Table 2 LAMBADA row)\n    (0.273 + 0.393) / 2,         # Pythia 70M within-seed\n    (0.348 + 0.310) / 2,         # within-arch (Appendix W table)\n]\n\n# Separate helped/hurt for error-bar-like annotation\nhelped = [0.05, 0.096, 0.273, 0.348]\nhurt   = [0.05, 0.091, 0.393, 0.310]\n\ncolors = ['#bbbbbb', '#d62728', '#E69F00', '#2ca02c']\n\nx = np.arange(len(categories))\nwidth = 0.55\n\nfig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(3.3, 3.0))\n\nbars = ax.bar(x, means, width, color=colors, alpha=0.85, zorder=3,\n              edgecolor='white', linewidth=0.5)\n\n# Add helped/hurt split indicators\nfor i in range(1, len(categories)):\n    ax.plot([i - 0.08, i - 0.08], [helped[i], hurt[i]], color='#333333',\n            linewidth=1.2, zorder=4)\n    ax.plot(i - 0.08, helped[i], '_', color='#333333', markersize=6, zorder=4)\n    ax.plot(i - 0.08, hurt[i], '_', color='#333333', markersize=6, zorder=4)\n\n# Value labels on bars\nfor i, (bar, m) in enumerate(zip(bars, means)):\n    ax.text(bar.get_x() + bar.get_width() / 2, m + 0.015,\n            f'{m:.2f}', ha='center', va='bottom', fontsize=9, fontweight='bold',\n            color=colors[i])\n\n# Multiplier brackets (clean, no arrows)\nbracket_y = 0.42\nax.plot([0, 0, 1, 1], [means[0]+0.02, bracket_y-0.06, bracket_y-0.06, means[1]+0.02],\n        color='#888888', lw=0.8)\nax.text(0.5, bracket_y - 0.05, '2×', ha='center', fontsize=8, color='#888888')\n\nax.plot([1, 1, 3, 3], [means[1]+0.02, bracket_y, bracket_y, means[3]+0.02],\n        color='#888888', lw=0.8)\nax.text(2.0, bracket_y + 0.005, '3×', ha='center', fontsize=8, color='#888888')\n\nax.set_xticks(x)\nax.set_xticklabels(categories)\nax.set_ylabel('Mean pairwise Jaccard $\\\\bar{J}$')\nax.set_ylim(0, 0.50)\n\n# Note about Pythia scale\nax.text(0.98, 0.97, 'LAMBADA, 1.3B\\n(Pythia: 70M)',\n        transform=ax.transAxes, fontsize=7.5, fontstyle='italic',\n        color='#555555', va='top', ha='right')\n\nout_dir = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))\nfig.savefig(os.path.join(out_dir, 'fig_jaccard_hierarchy.pdf'))\nfig.savefig(os.path.join(out_dir, 'fig_jaccard_hierarchy.png'), dpi=300)\nplt.close()\nprint('Saved fig_jaccard_hierarchy')\n",
    "userModified": false
  },

_source line 146 · 2026-05-24T05:30:01.947Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Good first iteration. Let me diagnose each figure:

**Figure 1 (Hero):**
Round 1 assessment:
- ✓ Font sizes appropriate for column width
- ✓ Clean design with proper spines removed
- ✓ CI bands not too dominant
- ✓ Independence baseline in panel (b)
- ✓ Good annotations
- Issues to fix:
  1. The "indep." label text is slightly cut off (needs better positioning)
  2. Panel (b) x-axis label says "Attention ratio (%)" but the tick labels don't have "%" - actually they do (4, 7.7, 14.3, 25) - but the axis label says (%) which is redundant with panel (a). 
  3. The "flat from 4%" annotation arrow is pointing slightly awkwardly
  4. The saturation band in panel (b) could be slightly more visible
  5. The CI bands for J(hurt) are quite wide and overlap with the saturation band
  6. The y-axis starts at 0.00 in panel (a) which wastes space at the bottom
  7. Looking at the overall composition, the two-panel layout actually works quite well at this size, so the figure is in pretty good shape.

Now moving to Figure 2, I'm checking the task specificity plot. The color palette and lollipop design look clean, the legend placement inside the figure works well, and the background shading is appropriately muted. I notice the rho/p text labels for both HellaSwag and ARC-Challenge are overlapping with their marker circles, which needs fixing. The annotation text at the bottom is small but acceptable for a footnote. Figure 3 is a heatmap that's coming together nicely with clean color coding and clear cells.

For the heatmap, the gray diagonal and white grid lines are working well, and the colorbar is good. The main issues I'm seeing are that the $J_h$ and $J_u$ labels are too faint and need more prominence to distinguish the triangles, the titles could be slightly smaller to avoid dominating the space, and the x-axis labels at 35° rotation work but could be less rotated. The color range from 0.04 to 0.16 is effective, and I notice the RetNet-DeltaNet cell in ARC-Easy correctly shows 0.14 in dark blue as the maximum value.

For the Jaccard Saturation figure, using direct labels instead of a legend is excellent, and the independence baseline reference line with good color differentiation and distinct line styles helps readability. The main issues are that the direct labels on the right side are slightly cramped with LAMBADA and PIQA overlapping vertically, the ARC-Easy label appears to be floating above the saturation band, and the "independence" label positioning at x=0.255 doesn't align well with where the line actually starts. The saturation band itself is subtle but visible, which works well overall.

For the Jaccard Hierarchy figure, the project palette colors are working nicely, the bar chart with value labels is clean, and the helped/hurt range is clear.

Now I'm moving into round 2 fixes, focusing on repositioning the "indep." label and improving annotation placement in Figure 1, fixing text overlap with markers in Figure 2, making the $J_h$/$J_u$ labels stand out more in Figure 3, and addressing the label overlaps and "independence" label positioning in Figure 4.
```

_source line 215 · 2026-05-24T05:33:17.776Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Round 3 assessment:

**Figure 1**: 
- ✓ "indep." label no longer overlaps with legend - but it's now just "indep." which is a bit cryptic. It's placed above the dotted line at x=24 which is fine.
- Overall the figure looks very clean and professional
- The two panels clearly show the decoupling narrative
- ✓ Good

**Figure 2**:
- The legend moved to lower right but NOW it overlaps with HellaSwag and ARC-Challenge text annotations. Specifically:


- HellaSwag and ARC-Challenge rho values are partially hidden behind the legend
- Need to reposition the legend to the upper right where there's more empty space, or make it more compact, or tuck it into an unused area

**Figure 3**:
- The $J_h$ and $J_u$ labels now have white background boxes positioned on the diagonal, which looks good
- The diagonal dashed line separating the triangles is a nice touch
- However, $J_h$ is overlapping with the "0.12" data value in the GDN-GLA cell in the ADA panel, and similarly overlaps with "0.11" in the ARC-Easy panel
- $J_u$ placement is slightly better but still causes overlap issues
- Best approach: either remove the in-figure labels entirely and explain them in the caption, or place them completely outside the data area

**Figure 4**:
- Labels are now well-separated with ARC-Easy at the top, LAMBADA and PIQA in the middle, and HellaSwag below
- LAMBADA and PIQA are still close but clearly distinguishable
- Overall design is clean

My fixes: move the Figure 2 legend to the upper right corner or make it horizontal at the top, and for Figure 3, relocate the $J_h$ and $J_u$ labels to empty space outside the matrix or remove them and rely on the caption instead.

For Figure 3, I think the cleanest solution is placing those labels outside the matrix bounds entirely—maybe along the sides or in a top corner above the data. The diagonal dashed line already communicates the triangle split visually, so the labels just need unambiguous positioning in empty space.

For Figure 2, I could color the y-axis tick labels according to their taxonomy and remove the legend entirely—this is cleaner than a separate legend and follows the principle of direct labeling over legends.
```

</details>

- ✓ Diagonal dashed line clearly separates upper/lower triangles
- ✓ Bottom annotation explains the convention
- ✓ Clean, professional heatmap
- The diagonal line crosses through some cells but it's subtle enough

Now I'm running through the final visual quality checklist across all five figures to ensure they meet the design standards: no matplotlib defaults, semantic color usage, readable text at column width, proper alignment, good whitespace, and refined details.

I need to verify that the data values in each figure actually match what's reported in the paper. Starting with Figure 1—checking the sigma values against Table 1 to confirm the scaling is correct. Now I'm verifying the correlation coefficients and p-values from Figure 2 against Table 1—LAMBADA and ARC-Easy both check out. For Figure 3, I'm confirming the heatmap values stay below the stated thresholds, and Figure 4 uses the same Jaccard data I've already validated.

Moving to Figure 5, I'm checking the baseline independence value at 0.05, then verifying the cross-architecture and within-architecture Jaccard similarities by averaging their reported pairs—all the calculations align with what's stated in the paper. Everything's checking out, so I'll update my progress and continue.

Before moving to compilation, I want to give Figure 1 one more polish since it's the main figure. Looking at the details: the "indep." label is quite small at 5pt and might strain readability, the confidence intervals in panel (b) are reasonably wide given the actual uncertainty, and the annotations like "-79%" and "rebound" are positioned well. The x-axis label "Attention ratio (%)" repeats across both panels, but matplotlib's subplot handling makes consolidating it awkward, so I'll keep it as is. The figures are looking solid overall.

Let me do a final check on Figure 5 since I haven't touched it since the first round—it looks good with the updated color palette now. Before compiling the paper, I should verify that all referenced figures actually exist in the figures directory, particularly fig5_per_example_bars.pdf which is in the figure list but I want to confirm it's been generated.

Actually, looking back at the user's figure list, Figure 5 is fig_jaccard_hierarchy.pdf, not fig5_per_example_bars.pdf, so that's what I've been working with. Now I need to verify all five output files are present in docs/paper/figures/.
